## Reproduced original VP9 color-metadata defect — unresolved

New tests/video-playback.spec.ts records a320x240 owned red page and six actual
clicks, then decodes the produced WebM and checks dimensions and a known pixel.
Original Chromium passes; Firefox fails the unchanged R>=230/G<=20/B<=20/A=255
assertion: actual pixel[255,24,0,255], readyState4, dimensions320x240. A numeric-only
diagnostic retains the failure. Both Chromium- and Firefox-produced recordings
decode[254,0,0,255] in Chromium and[255,24,0,255] in Firefox, before and after
playback. Captured owned processes are independently zero after each decoder.

ffprobe reports VP9/yuv420p/tv and unspecified color matrix. Container-only
transfer/primaries tags do not change Firefox pixels. A metadata-only VP9 header
filter setting BT.601 makes both engines decode[254,0,0,255]; decoded raw YUV420p
frame hashes are identical before/after, not a claim of byte-identical packets.
A temporary installed-bundle encoder control adding -colorspace smpte170m makes
the actual two-engine MCP test pass2/2 without assertion/budget changes. The
dependency is restored byte-for-byte in finally; restored Node22 Firefox fails
again. Both baseline and failing restored run remain failed acceptance evidence.

Pinned1.64.1 and read-only upstream main have the same videoRecorder.ts blob;
the public Screencast/MCP video options expose no color-space/encoder argument.
A one-line upstream patch proposal is retained, not published or shipped. No
vendored bundle, hidden artifact rewrite, runtime monkeypatch or system-FFmpeg
dependency introduced. New regression remains failing; this is an additional P2
video acceptance blocker, not a cause/fix claim for older capture/lifecycle failures.
WebKit/native new frame checks remain unexecuted locally/pending. Generated
recordings remain under/tmp, not committed. Strict typecheck passes. Evidence:
native83-video-proof.json, full decoder/negative/control logs and source hashes.

## Defects and risk register

- QA-STDERR-001 (P1): original source-client fixture retained an unbounded stderr
  string. Before real child1,048,742 retained bytes accepted; local fixed1MiB
  growing byte capture rejects overflow/unchecked EOF, drains pipes, awaits all
  closes. Unit9 eachLTS and real positive/negative regressions pass locally; native
  verification pending. No product transport/RAM/default/budget claim.

- RISK-001/002: runtime SDK promoted, lock regenerated, source/tarball checks implemented.
- RISK-003: inherited publish operations removed; private fork package/registry identities.
- RISK-004: implicit loopback removed; exact local policy. Newly reproduced P0:
  route.continue follows redirects without re-interception. Fixed using route.fetch
  maxRedirects:0/maxRetries:0; **all redirects currently rejected**, including approved
  same-origin. WebSockets closed, service workers blocked. DNS/OS egress still residual.
- RISK-005: supervised CLI/MCP lifecycle/report deadlines and scoped process
  termination exist; raw runner remains cooperative. Artifact cleanup now has an
  independent5s worker/1s kill confirmation; full native acceptance and durable
  maintenance uncertainty remain open.
- RISK-006: per-run private artifacts, native66 five-lane20-run identity proof,
  payload ceilings and retained-artifact admission budgets exist. Explicit age-based
  confirmed-job pruning validates natively. Admission is not a hard disk/global
  quota; automatic scheduling and screenshot pixel masking remain open.
- RISK-007: fixed HTTP/action/cleanup/journal taxonomies and redacted receipts exist.
  Main HTTP error-before-readiness precision controls pass native63 onward;
  native browser failures and diagnostic coverage remain incomplete.
- RISK-008: durable jobs/queue/status/result/reconnect-without-replay, foreground
  CLI and protected Windows storage/public clients exist. Automatic resume,
  uncertain-lease operator recovery and full lifecycle acceptance remain open.
- RISK-009: Chromium/Firefox verified selectively; WebKit libraries unavailable locally.
- RISK-010: blocked requests produce policy_blocked, not false workflow success.
- RISK-011: unknown fields rejected, actions copied before async execution to prevent
  mutation-after-validation URL bypass (test reproduced before fix). Schema v1 and 1 MiB bound added; deeper AST/assertion semantics pending.
- RISK-012: read-only workflow permissions, concurrency cancellation, timeouts and OS
  browser matrix added; actual green runs still required.
- RISK-013: route.fetch/fulfill changes network semantics/cache; benchmarks incomplete.
- RISK-014: docs remove implicit-localhost/runtime-SDK claims; unsupported features explicit.
- Dependency audit initially found 3 advisories (1 low, 2 moderate); compatible npm audit
  fix updated transitive lock entries. Recheck recorded in evidence.

Native WebKit missing libgtk-4, libgraphene, libharfbuzz-icu, libmanette,
libhyphen, libGLESv2; cannot install with local su authentication. Branch protection
API 403. These do not excuse unimplemented jobs/schema/security tests.

NEXT ACTION: inspect CI; fix actual failures, then implement isolated worker job
execution to enforce full lifecycle/process termination and durable status before
adding wider workflow actions. Keep all gates incomplete until independently proved.

## Milestone 2 — ongoing

- Screenshot capture now disabled unless operator option allowSensitiveArtifacts
  is true, CLI --allow-sensitive-artifacts or MCP environment
  PLAYWRIGHT_BATCH_ALLOW_SENSITIVE_ARTIFACTS=1. Workflow/page cannot enable it.
- 20 real screenshots across paired concurrent Chromium runs passed uniqueness
  and POSIX 0700 directory checks (artifacts.test.mjs). Retention still absent.
- Actual EOF and SIGTERM during a hanging real browser navigation each failed
  before fix (>10s no exit). SDK stdio transport does not handle stdin EOF itself.
  Explicit EOF/signal server close + active abort fixed both: 2/2 passed under 1s.
  Process-tree enumeration/forced-kill proof still outstanding.
- Schema v1 (unversioned legacy treated as v1), 1 MiB serialized input cap, strict
  role/name/testId/label/placeholder/text selectors, deep selector snapshot added.
- CI first milestone: Linux/macOS wrapper and batch jobs succeeded when inspected;
  Windows/Docker still running. No full green claim yet.
- Removed redundant feat-branch push trigger: PR plus main push only; cancelled
  duplicate push run 39474710658 deliberately (NOT a functional failure).

## Milestone 3 — current work

Code milestone 2 SHA: `32477dd9cdf62a3664ac9427149cec39b9b0a603`.
Native CI on that SHA: Linux/macOS batch 22/22 passed; Windows 21/22 passed,
with an invalid POSIX-only exit-code expectation for abrupt Windows SIGTERM.
Fixed test to require signal SIGTERM/code null on Windows; EOF still requires
exit zero on all systems. This proves bounded server exit, not process-tree cleanup.

Wrapper matrix on same SHA: Linux/macOS/Docker passed; Windows 35/36 passed.
Chrome cold startup took ~24s; first click exceeded default 5s stable-element
wait. Added explicit Windows Chrome test budget: action 15s/test 60s, and one Windows CI browser worker to avoid competing
cold branded Chrome launches. Linux/macOS retain two CI workers. Production
defaults unchanged; cold Windows Chrome reliability remains an investigation item
until fresh CI and repeated evidence. No automatic action retries added.

- Original storage/cookies, screenshot PNG bytes, trace file existence, video WebM
  bytes, tab management and Chromium PDF bytes now tested in tests/advanced.spec.ts.
  Local Chromium/Firefox: 2 passed; native WebKit/Windows evidence pending.

## Supervised execution candidate (native CI pending)

This candidate replaces CLI/MCP in-process execution with an independent watchdog
and execution worker. Public BrowserServer process ownership enables scoped
termination without exposing its private loopback endpoint. Deadlines cover setup,
actions, report publication and worker exit; cancellation, worker/supervisor crashes,
CPU stalls, stalled reporting and abrupt frontend death have fault fixtures.
Temporary browser directories are private and removed after termination. Atomic
report publication prevents partial JSON after injected ENOSPC; completed actions
are preserved as a lower bound and never automatically replayed. MCP blocks new
work after uncertain cleanup. Direct raw runner calls retain cooperative semantics.

Evidence under `docs/evidence/worker-*`:
- Latest targeted lifecycle acceptance: 9 passed, 0 failed/skipped/cancelled.
- Complete local batch suite: 38 tests, 35 passed, 3 failed, no skips/cancellations.
  All three failures are WebKit setup due to missing host libraries in this
  container; these are failures, not passes or evidence of native compatibility.
- Node 22 subset: 9 passed (executor, report fault and MCP 1,000 operations).
- Independently installed production package in a path containing spaces:
  both endpoints completed real browser calls; original discovered 25 tools,
  companion one. Lint and diff whitespace checks pass.
- Preserved failing-before fixtures prove the report partial-write and startup
  cancellation race before their fixes. New native Windows/macOS/Linux CI is
  required before assigning compatibility evidence to this runtime candidate.

Remaining boundaries: CLI file loading/parsing is outside the supervised budget;
256 MiB is worker V8 heap, not total RSS; route.fetch buffers allowed responses;
DNS/OS egress and disk/response quotas remain open. Process termination is not an
OS sandbox and cannot promise recovery from kernel stalls or simultaneous host
controller loss. Atomic rename is not a fsync-backed durable job journal. No
queues, durable jobs or persistent companion profiles are implemented. G06/G07
and the full mission remain incomplete; old 212-test evidence applies to 2321179.
NEXT ACTION: native candidate CI, bounded CLI ingestion, then remaining egress,
resource, durable-state and acceptance requirements. No publish or main merge.

## Follow-up: bounded input and successful long-poll cleanup

CLI file ingestion now opens without POSIX FIFO blocking, checks the opened
handle is a regular file, reads at most 1 MiB plus one sentinel byte, rejects
oversize/growth and invalid UTF-8, and supports a UTF-8 BOM. It does not promise
bounded latency for a stalled filesystem; ingestion still precedes execution's
monotonic deadline. Tests cover exact-limit JSON, oversize, invalid encoding,
directory and POSIX FIFO inputs.

context closure. Teardown now distinguishes transport errors after closing starts;

26-operation redaction/report fixture passed. Native CI for the preceding worker
commit remains pending; no new compatibility gate is claimed.

fresh installed-package real browser calls passed for both endpoints (58 files).

## Windows watchdog survival defect identified

At 1336a4b, Linux and macOS batch jobs each ran 43/43 successfully; Windows ran
41/43, with abrupt SIGTERM/host-death temporary cleanup failures and no cleanup
receipt. EOF and all three real browser deadline fixtures passed. To obtain the
run archive while macOS was queued, cancellation was requested. macOS acquired a
runner during that request and actually completed 43/43; overall batch run is
CANCELLED, not green. Wrapper Linux/Windows/Docker/lint passed; macOS wrapper is
CANCELLED. All outcomes and the failed Windows assertions are preserved.

Root cause from Node 20's bundled libuv source: non-detached children enter a
kill-on-parent-close Windows job object. The watchdog used detached:false on
Windows, so frontend death killed it before it could clean files; larger cleanup
budgets alone could not fix that. Source excerpt and exact upstream SHA are in
worker-windows-libuv-ownership.txt. The new candidate makes only the supervisor
detached on every platform; workers and cleanup children remain attached to their
Windows supervisor. Local lifecycle/protocol subset: 12 passed. Native Windows
survival remains pending; no all-OS or release PASS is claimed.

The same candidate includes the independently reproduced oversized-stdin shutdown
fix and next CI runs actual bootstrap scripts, plus complete Linux Node 22/24
batch jobs alongside three native Node 20 jobs. This is validation expansion,
not existing evidence of an LTS or bootstrap PASS.

## Durable cancellation/deadline success fence

A failing-before state test demonstrated SUCCEEDED could commit after a durable
cancel intent. finish now rejects success when cancellation was requested or the
stored deadline has passed. The caller must finish the appropriate failure state
after cleanup; rejection does not silently release a lease. A trusted test-only
clock fixture advances time at the finish command, proving deadline rejection
without native scheduling sleeps. Eleven tests pass on Node 24 and Node 22; lint
and diff checks pass. This remains a storage component, not a public jobs API.

## Trusted worker admission boundary (local verification)

Added source-only beforeWorkerStart to the supervised executor. The watchdog
withholds the worker start message until ownership admission resolves; rejection
returns journal_unavailable, pending admission cannot evade the existing deadline,
and a late resolution cannot start actions after cancellation/termination. No new
MCP/CLI inputs or job tools are exposed. Pre-ack failures preserve exact zero
actions. This is preparation for guardian-owned durable execution, not that full
integration. Default no-hook execution retains the direct start path.

## Internal durable execution bridge and cleanup-stage diagnostics

Added the trusted guardian described in JOBS_GUARDIAN.md. It verifies a canonical
workflow digest against the keyed journal record, activates its lease, commits
worker ownership before action admission, handles cancellation/IPC loss, and
persists only safe bounded results after cleanup. A guardian crash retains an
uncertain lease; no persisted PID authorizes a kill and no action resumes.
Six guardian + eleven journal + two snapshot tests pass locally (19) on Node
24 and Node 22. Fresh production tarball: both real browser endpoints plus a
real seven-action Chromium guardian workflow, durable result and owner isolation
pass. This remains an internal bridge: public jobs/queue/controller are absent.

although the guardian was already gone. The harness now observes exit and audits
descendants independently; failing-before and corrected logs are retained.

worker effects. Workflow fingerprints ignore object key order/defaults/repeat
expansion; source input mutation cannot change a captured workflow.

The raw runner now returns at most three fixed cleanup-stage codes (resource
context/browser/server, timeout/error). It continues to fail on cooperative
cleanup error; these diagnostics do not promote success. Two fault cases prove

## Guardian startup IPC-loss fence before native push

A trusted pre-disconnected-loader fixture reproduced a real startup race: the
old candidate missed the disconnect event during ESM loading, activated its lease
and ran the fake worker until TIMED_OUT. The guardian now explicitly checks
process.connected at startup and before processing a start packet, in addition
to its disconnect listener. The same fixture now becomes CANCELLED with exact
zero before activation; no worker effects start. All 20 component tests pass on
Node 24 and all 20 on Node 22 (zero failures/skips/cancellations). This remains
local fixture evidence; native and actual future controller SIGKILL proof remain.
The focused legacy Node 20 Firefox test also passes locally on Linux: one test,
five fresh complete workflows, no retries/skips. The Windows source cleanup
intermittency is not declared fixed.

## Bounded navigation throttling candidate (2026-10-21)

Source base c3183fe. `local-batch/navigation-retry.mjs` and runner routing add an
operator-only opt-in for known-safe top-level GET goto retry after 429/503.
Default is disabled. Maximum two retries, bounded exponential backoff/jitter,
Retry-After minimum, no shortening excessive server waits, and the existing
cancelable global deadline. No click, form/POST, subresource, redirect, network
exception or other-status retry. Each route.fetch keeps maxRetries/maxRedirects=0.
CLI --navigation-retries and MCP PLAYWRIGHT_BATCH_NAVIGATION_RETRIES accept 0–2;
workflow/tool JSON cannot enable the policy. GET replay safety requires operator
knowledge; this is not an assertion that all GET endpoints are harmless.

bound pending snapshots. Queue deadlines currently use wall-clock timestamps;
monotonic handling across clock changes remains open before public acceptance.

## Atomic no-overwrite artifact publication and temporary-file ownership

Two deterministic regressions expose a publication race: screenshot/report target
created after the existence check/during private temporary I/O is replaced by
rename. Both tests fail before the fix (unexpected success). Reports also delete
a pre-existing temporary-name collision in unconditional finally cleanup; the
new ownership test fails before with ENOENT reading the marker it must preserve.
The fixtures use only private owned directories and static synthetic values.

Screenshot/report publication now atomically links the complete private temporary
file to an absent final name and removes its own temporary link. EEXIST preserves
the competing destination. Reports acquire an exclusive FileHandle first, track
creation ownership, close after success/partial writes, and unlink only a temporary
file they created. The existing real-browser partial ENOSPC fixture is adapted
to the FileHandle write boundary; the fault is not removed or weakened. No action
is replayed and failed publication cannot become job success.

Node20/24 each pass7 unit cases: simultaneous captures have one complete winner,
racing destinations and unowned temporary collisions survive, reports are complete
and summaries remain bounded, private POSIX mode and final link count1 hold.
Node22 real Chromium report-ENOSPC/screenshot quota/CLI cases pass3; public jobs
MCP1,000-action/owner/dedup/cancel/reconnect/cleanup/quota harness passes1 in41.147s.
Fresh production-only141-file package passes real original/batch/jobs MCP/CLI,
profile guard, durable controller/guardian/journal and controller-SIGKILL recovery
with zero active owned processes. Evidence:artifact-publication-* and the three
*-before records. These are per-command counts, not a combined unique total.

Publication requires filesystem hard-link support inside the artifact directory,
as documented in LOCAL_BATCH.md. Unsupported filesystems fail closed. Normal
completion leaves a single private final name; crashes during the brief two-link
window can require conservative operator cleanup. This does not implement full
project/browser/profile disk quotas or retention. Native acceptance is pending.

## Active listener attribution and full storage-tool positives

The new optional verify-active-listeners.mjs waits for a real supervised Chromium
navigation to complete, then attributes TCP socket inodes only to eight live owned
processes while a locator wait holds execution active. Linux Node22 and24 each
observe exactly one owned listener, on the execution worker at exact127.0.0.1;
all eight processes' FD links are readable in both snapshots. Cancellation then
confirms one completed action, terminated/removed cleanup and zero owned survivors.
This is point-in-time TCP evidence, not DNS/UDP/egress isolation or a native OS
listener audit. The script reports partial coverage explicitly if browser FD access
is denied; unreadable worker descriptors fail rather than imply a missing listener.
Evidence: `evidence/active-listener-audit-node22.json` and
`evidence/active-listener-audit-node24.json`.

New original storage capability tests exercise all15 cookie/localStorage/
sessionStorage get/set/list/delete/clear tools against an owned isolated browser.
Two seeded values are visible; deleting one preserves its sibling, and clear removes
both. Six local Chromium/Firefox cases pass(10.8s), including browser_close ACK.
Evidence: `evidence/original-storage-local.txt`. This is expanded positive behavior
coverage, not a claim that all72 tool behaviors or native variants are complete.
No delegated upstream implementation or companion code surface changed.

## Controller-death verifier ordering: reproduced and corrected

The verifier previously created its replacement journal worker before old execution
shutdown, then asserted zero processes immediately after observing a terminal row.
Guardian finish commits before its own journal close/exit. A trusted fixture holds
only post-disconnect bookkeeping for500ms; the original verifier deterministically
fails with three recorded processes. This is a fixture-only delay after real DB
close, not a workflow action delay. Corrected ordering waits for zero old processes
within the existing10s bound before creating the replacement observer, then reads
CANCELLED, requires no operator attention, proves the next lease and SQLite integrity.
It also avoids a new observer reusing an old recorded PID during the old-tree audit.
Initial Windows roots are registered only while live; identity guards remain strict.

The same lingering-close case passes locally with700ms shutdown; Node22/24 real
regressions each pass. Fresh154-file production install passes regular252ms and
lingering824ms shutdown checks, zero owned survivors and released lease in both.
The installed verifier now exercises both modes. No runtime success is manufactured
from failed cleanup. Prior macOS survivor identity was not captured, so this does
not assert which process that old PID represented. Native confirmation remains open.

filter to that allowlist. Signal targets, enumeration, deadlines, exit checks and
boolean success criteria are unchanged. Callback exceptions cannot change cleanup.

970-action cleanup failure.

## Read-only original profile diagnosis

`playwright-mcp profile-doctor <directory>` reuses the existing profile lease
reader before loading the browser backend. It inspects only bounded guard metadata
and root permission metadata, classifies missing/unreadable locks and recorded PID
presence, and reports identityVerified:false. A recorded PID is not incarnation
proof or authority for reset/termination. Tokens, PIDs, paths and credentials are
not printed. Windows ACL privacy remains explicitly unverified. An absent guard
is not proof that an unrelated browser is not using the directory.

The command does not create a missing profile or repair permissions, reset state,
release a lock or kill a process. Existing acquire/release behavior is unchanged.
Nine local tests pass on each of Node20/22/24: real active leases, a dead owner,
malformed/oversized/link metadata, unreadable fake credential bytes, permission
preservation, no stdout canaries and rejected reset arguments. Evidence:
profile-doctor-node20/22/24.txt. This is scoped diagnosis for scenario35, not a
new profile store or completed managed-profile/reset/retention acceptance.

Fresh155-file production-only install passes the read-only doctor on an actual
guarded persistent profile, without exposing token/path, and confirms absent guard
after close, cookie restoration, contention and zero owned survivors. Both normal
and lingering controller-death checks pass. Evidence: profile-doctor-production-package.txt.

## Observed open-page budget

The companion now defaults to eight observed open pages per isolated workflow
context, including the main page. The operator may choose1–32 using CLI
--max-pages, PLAYWRIGHT_BATCH_MAX_PAGES or PLAYWRIGHT_JOBS_MAX_PAGES. Source API
maxPages is trusted host configuration. Workflow/tool JSON cannot raise it.
Invalid values fail before worker startup or job storage creation. Existing
original Microsoft tools and discovery schemas are unchanged.

A context page event exceeding the budget interrupts the workflow with fixed
resource_limit/page_quota metadata, suppresses further steps and late screenshot
publication, and keeps actionsSucceeded false. Effects may already have happened;
retrySafe remains false and duplicate request keys do not replay. Confirmed owned
cleanup permits the next lease; uncertain cleanup still blocks it. Closed page
events release observed slots. This is reactive: allocation may precede the event;
it is not an OS CPU/RAM/process quota or a global cross-project limit.

A real owned three-popup regression previously ignored maxPages2 and continued
to the next navigation (one Chromium failure retained). Corrected maxPages4
positive control and maxPages2 rejection pass Chromium/Firefox on Node20 and24;

macOS trusted100-click close, large-DOM interaction and storage restore. Existing
SDK disconnect errors can omit the pending tool. Test clients now track only
versioned allowlisted tool names, monotonic durations and fixed outcome labels.
At unexpected test failure, before teardown, emit at most four clients with four
active and four recent calls each. No argument/result/error text, URL, path, PID,
client name or source code enters the receipt; unknown tool names become
unknown_tool. Calls preserve their original receiver, arguments, return values and
exceptions. No extra MCP request or per-call I/O is added, and no production tool
schema/backend or deadline changes.

fixture intentionally exits with one failed test and confirms that its unknown
name/argument canaries are absent from the emitted receipt; that temporary test

affected original Chromium/Firefox cases pass(41.3s), and a Node20 fixture/client
loading cookie case passes.164-file dry pack includes both QA helper files; the
last162-file production-only verification covers unchanged runtime entry points.

## Independent artifact cleanup containment (local candidate)

Reused existing count/depth/bytes/owner/link scan in a separate child, rather than
changing deletion semantics. Parent supplies only its authorized execution scope;
worker has no browser/workflow/shell input. Fixed IPC receipts omit paths/content.
A five-second filesystem budget plus one-second kill confirmation bounds waiting
on this controller's event loop. A positive receipt alone never succeeds: natural
exit0 must follow. Timeout/malformed/double receipt/error produces fixed failure
and targets only the actual ChildProcess handle. Unconfirmed termination never
claims success and blocks subsequent scopes/new jobs in the same controller.
This is not durable cross-controller maintenance tracking or a verified guarantee
after parent death. Partial file removal remains possible on timeout.

Faults cover pre-result CPU hang, positive-result CPU hang, malformed/private
receipt, fixed rejection and signal exit; an unrelated live sibling survives.
Normal worker preview/delete/absent scope and safe errors pass. Native validation
of this new adapter remains pending; six MCP schemas are unchanged.

Component receipts: Node22/24 each59/59 before the final same-controller guard;
final changed boundaries (CLI/retention/containment)17/17 each. Node20 final
boundaries11/11. A queued operation rechecks the unconfirmed latch before reading
its scope, so already-enqueued cleanup also stops. Fresh installed production169-file package
passes original/batch/jobs/profile/guardian smoke checks. Lint and diff checks
pass. No native evidence yet for this unpushed adapter.

The controller bounds queued journal operations, but those slots are released
between a get_workflow_result call's polls. Thus arbitrarily many dormant waits
could remain for up to10s each. A real SDK/SQLite five-wait control fails before
the fix (zero busy instead of one). jobs-mcp.mjs now admits at most four positive
waitMs calls per server. Excess gets existing fixed controller_busy; status,
cancel and zero-wait reads do not consume wait slots. Finally releases on normal
completion, unknown job or error/abort. No schema/deadline/SQLite migration,
automatic retry or broad hard OS-memory quota claim. Docs explain this bound.

Final targeted SDK case passes1/1 on Node22/24, no skips/cancellations: synthetic
queued metadata (no browser execution), five simultaneous waits yield four
cancelled-job results/one busy; cancel remains available, completed and not_found
lookups both release slots for four fresh calls. The full Node24 endpoint file
passes2/2 in44.503s, including real Chromium1k, owner isolation, dedup, cancellation,
EOF/reconnect and independent process-scope audit. That full run precedes the
last added not_found-slot assertion, which is verified by final targeted runs.
Fresh Node22 production172 install exits0 with both independent parent-death
receipts. Lint/diff pass; evidence jobs-wait-* separates before and final checks.

held. Ordinary run logs await completion; completed macOS job log API redirects
returned403 (no workaround/retry). Check annotations only say exit1; no test

## Temporary cleanup helper — bounded failure and drained receipts (local)

The old timeout killed its owned child but waited without a finite confirmation;
IPC errors could return without stopping it, null receipts threw, and exit-before-
final-IPC could lose success. The helper now allows1s owned-handle confirmation
following the existing operation deadline, disconnects/unrefs when unconfirmed,
and returns false without claiming termination. Success requires strict single
boolean receipt, drained close0 and monotonic deadline. Repeated errors are handled.
No stored-PID kill, lease release, action replay or expanded action budget.

Five isolated fault controls cover missing exit, repeated errors, malformed receipt,
exit-before-final-IPC and late close despite delayed timer delivery. Real worker
parent-EOF control suspends JS filesystem completion and audits owned descendants;
it does not prove kernel-uninterruptible I/O termination. Final components85/85
Node22/24 and focused7/7 Node20 pass; production173 install and both normal/linger
controller-death audits report zero observed owned processes. Lint/diff pass.
Evidence temp-stop-* preserves before failures and final receipts separately.
Native57 predates this change; cross-platform proof remains pending.

pipe1k/1k98.736s; Codex failure-only probe pipe1k/1k100.118s versus server963/1k
timeout. Each pipe captured/spawned/exited one public-channel owned handle; all
comparison audits observed zero owned processes. Windows private Firefox10/10
(230 actions), inherited1 successful fresh run then next failed after4 actions
(27 cumulative). This sample does not establish a universal pipe fix, Windows

## Retained artifact admission (local, follows a178e5b/005398f)

JobController.open now sets a512MiB retained logical-byte default on its standard
private owner/project directory. Trusted source callers may choose1byte–1GiB;

the supervised worker scans existing retained entries and reserves the entire
screenshot allowance +8MiB report +16KiB fixed diagnostic allowance. More than

worker deadlines bound stalled scanning; the project lease serializes jobs/cleanup.
